<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The consistency check found 3 CRITICALs:

1. **0.73 in abstract not in results** — abstract says "surface-only (0.73)" but results.tex says "surface-only features (TTR + hapax; F1=0.729)". 0.73 is the rounded version of 0.729. This is fine — the abstract uses 2 decimal places (0.73) and results uses 3 (0.729).


2. **0.75 in abstract not in results** — abstract says "reference-only (0.75)" but results.tex says "reference-only features (entropy statistics + tail decay; F1=0.745)". Same rounding issue — 0.75 is just the rounded form of 0.745.
3. **\\cite{placeholder} in method_01fe38** — This is a backup file, not the active method.tex, so it's not actually a problem.

All three are false positives. The first two are just rounding differences between the abstract's 2-decimal precision and the results' 3-decimal precision, and the third is in a backup file that's not being used.

The paper checks out. Now I need to submit it to the Review team for EMNLP. I'm thinking about whether to use "fresh" mode or "continue" mode — the system shows the project is already in review stage with an existing review chat, so this would be a continuation to verify all the fixes are in place rather than a completely fresh review.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/introduction_4bbcfd.tex:20:We study distributional drift in non-paraphrastic LLM generation chains across six generators from three model families (Pythia, OLMo, Qwen; 0.6B--8B parameters).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/introduction_4bbcfd.tex:34:    \item \textbf{Empirical characterization.} We document differential depth response across six generators spanning three model families (0.6B--8B): a 7-feature ensemble combining drifting and stationary features achieves LOGO F1\,=\,0.79, significantly outperforming all single-detector baselines ($p < 0.001$) with tight cross-generator consistency.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/main_53f11a.tex:22:We formulate \emph{ordinal generation depth estimation}---classifying text into depth classes (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+) rather than producing a binary label---and study distributional drift in non-paraphrastic LLM generation chains across six generators from three model families (0.6B--8B parameters).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/main.tex:26:In leave-one-generator-out evaluation across 6 generators from 3 model families (0.6B--8.2B parameters), the ensemble achieves macro-F1 of 0.79 ($p < 0.001$ vs.\ all single-detector baselines), with per-generator held-out F1 ranging from 0.68 to 0.86.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/conclusion.tex:5:Across six generators from three model families (0.6B--8.2B parameters), we documented differential depth response: central-tendency and diversity features drift monotonically with generation depth, while distributional shape features remain near-stationary.

🛠️ **响应方式**：本条催促**必须**通过调用 `keepalive_respond` 工具回复。
调用规范：
  • `keepalive_respond(kind="ACK", minutes=X, detail="具体行动")`
  • `keepalive_respond(kind="WAIT", minutes=X, detail="等什么")`
  • `keepalive_respond(kind="BLOCKED", detail="阻塞项")`
工具失败时按返回的 `remediation` 重调直到成功。不调用工具 = 视为本轮无响应（连续 2 次升级运维群）。

---

⏰ **[System → Director]**

论文 8/10 收敛、7 项修正完成、编译通过——进展不错，辛苦了。几个点跟进一下：

**GPU 全闲。** 4 张卡分配给你，0 张在跑。全部实验都结束了我理解，但 ROADMAP 还有待完成项——"6-gen bootstrap CI 补算"和"fig1/fig2 完整数据重新生成"，这些不正好可以现在用闲置卡跑？你标了"可选"和"非必须"，但卡反正空着，跑了只会让论文更扎实。跟 Review Agent 确认一下这两个值不值得补上。

**Review Director fresh 审查。** 你 06:58 说已提交审查，现在 30 分钟过去了，审查回来了吗？如果还没回来，你在等的同时可以做什么？DDL 是 5/25，还有 10 天，时间充裕但不是无限的。

**论文质量最大化。** 8/10 是投稿就绪，但不是天花板。23 个成功实验、effect size 递减、entropy 跃迁、8B scale 验证、prompt type 覆盖——素材很丰富。趁等审查的时间，跟 Review Agent 认真想想：claim 能不能更强？还有没有低成本高收益的 ablation 可以补？目标是最大化分数，不是"刚好够投"。

**等审查 ≠ 停工。** 你和 Agent、Reviewer 都是 AI，写作和 coding 效率很高。审查没回来之前，能并行推进的事情先推进。

  "text": "**GPU 空闲利用 — 两个并行任务**\n\n4 张 GPU 全空，趁等 Review Director 审查，跑两个可选但有价值的任务：\n\n**任务 A — 6-gen bootstrap CI 补算**（cuda:0）\n当前论文中 6-gen LOGO 0.789 没有 bootstrap CI。用现有的 features_all_models.csv 跑 1000 次 bootstrap 重采样计算 6-gen LOGO 的 95% CI。结果用于替换 Table 1 和 Table 3 中的 CI 值。脚本在服务器上 `/root/gen-depth-fingerprint/` 目录。\n\n**任务 B — fig1/fig2 完整数据重生成**（cuda:1）\n当前 fig1（differential response）和 fig2（LOGO performance）用的是 82% snapshot 数据。用 100% 完整数据重新生成。预期差异 <0.002，但用完整数据更严谨。图表输出到 `artifacts/_project/paper/` 对应位置。\n\n两个任务独立，分别起 Worker 并行跑。完成后汇报结果。"
}

_source line 2966 · 2026-05-15T07:33:12.621Z_

[Reviewer 回复]:
## Claim 强度 + 低成本 Ablation 评估

### 一、Claim 强度评估

**结论：当前 claim 强度与证据匹配，不建议 upgrade。**

逐条分析：

**C1 (Depth Discriminability)** — 措辞"LOGO F1=0.79, per-generator 0.68–0.86"已是实际数据。可以考虑更强调"跨家族泛化"（LOFO 仅降 3.7pp），但论文已在 Table 1 脚注中写了。升级为"robust"或"generalizable"的风险：Qwen3-8B 的 0.683 是明显短板，审稿人会用它来攻击"robust"这个词。**维持现状。**

**C2 (Differential Depth Response)** — shape stationarity 已加 "under continuation prompts" 限定。如果去掉限定升级为 universal pattern，与 plan-005 数据矛盾（elaboration/rewriting 下 shape 无信息量）。**维持现状。**

**C3 (Negative Finding)** — diagnostic vs curative 定位精确，negative finding 无法也不应 upgrade。**维持现状。**

**唯一可微调之处**：paper_framing.md 的 headline template 写 "macro-F1 of 0.82"，但论文实际用 6-gen LOGO 0.789→0.79。framing 文档本身需要同步更新（0.82 是旧的 plan-001 单模型 CV 数字）。这不是论文问题，是 framing doc 滞后。

**总体判断**：Claims 越保守越不容易被 reject。当前强度下审稿人能攻击的主要角度（scale gap, prompt-type 依赖, lab chains）都已在 limitations 中诚实陈述。升级 claim 只会暴露更多攻击面。

---

### 二、低成本 Ablation 机会

| # | 实验 | GPU-hours | 收益 | 评级 |
|---|------|-----------|------|------|
| 1 | 5-class 深度分类结果 | **0h**（数据已存在） | 预堵 RC3 最可能的审稿人问题 | **[推荐]** |
| 2 | Leave-one-feature-out ablation | <0.5h | 补充当前粗粒度 ablation（2 vs 5 vs 7），显示每个特征的边际贡献 | **[推荐]** |
| 3 | LOGO 主结果多 seed 稳定性 | <0.1h | 当前仅报 bootstrap CI，XGBoost 随机种子方差未报 | **[推荐]** |
| 4 | 混淆矩阵（appendix） | **0h** | 展示 gen-1 vs gen-2+ 混淆模式，增加可解释性 | **[推荐]** |
| 5 | 替代分类器（LogReg / RF） | <0.5h | 证明 signal 在特征不在分类器，preempt "换个 classifier 呢？" | **[可选]** |
| 6 | XGBoost 超参敏感性 | <0.5h | 20-50 组超参 grid，证明结果不依赖特定调参 | **[可选]** |
| 7 | 更多 generator / cross-model chains | >5h + 代码 | 收益大但成本高，DDL 前不现实 | **[不值得]** |

#### 具体建议：

**#1 [推荐] 5-class 深度分类**：服务器上 `multi_classify_results.json` 已有 5-class（depth 0-4）LOGO 结果。只需整理成 appendix 表格。Limitations 第 5 条说 "we do not evaluate this possibility"——如果数据已有，这句话就是自己打自己脸。报告 5-class 结果（即使 F1 较低）能展示 depth resolution 的上限，是审稿人 RC3 最直接的回答。**0 GPU cost，纯写作。**

**#2 [推荐] Leave-one-feature-out**：当前 ablation 只有 surface(2) vs reference(5) vs full(7)。7 个 LOGO 评估（每次去掉 1 个特征），<30 分钟。期望发现：entropy_min（importance 0.015）和 tail_decay（0.012）去掉后几乎无损 → 可以在论文中明确说"7 features are all contributory, though 2 low-importance features have marginal impact"。

**#3 [推荐] 多 seed 稳定性**：用 5 个不同 XGBoost random_state 重跑 LOGO，报告 F1 的 mean±std。如果 std < 0.005，说明结果不依赖种子——这是当前论文缺失的一个 reproducibility 证据。

**#4 [推荐] 混淆矩阵**：从现有 LOGO 预测中提取 3×3 混淆矩阵放 appendix。展示 gen-1 的错误主要来自与 gen-0 混淆还是与 gen-2+ 混淆——这对理解"gen-1 is hardest"提供解释性支撑。

**#5-6 [可选]** 替代分类器和超参敏感性：有时间就做，优先级低于 #1-4。

**总工时估算**：#1-4 全做 ≈ 0.5 GPU·h + 2h 写作时间。完全在 DDL 内可控。

27:**Headline result template**: "On multi-generator continuation chains across 4 domains, our distributional fingerprint features achieve macro-F1 of 0.82 for 3-class depth estimation (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+), with leave-one-generator-out generalization at 0.80. Depth-aware data curation does not outperform random subsampling for downstream CLM quality, establishing depth fingerprinting as a diagnostic tool for provenance characterization, not a curative tool for data selection."
31:**RC1 — "Is this just vocabulary impoverishment?"**: Surface features alone achieve 0.76 macro-F1, but full features achieve 0.82 (p<0.001). We don't claim reference features are "better" — we show they capture a **different, complementary** signal (stationary distributional shape vs drifting level/diversity). The dual dynamics finding is itself a scientific contribution. Note: "fingerprint" in the paper refers to the full feature vector's combination, not just reference-model features.
```

          " **Our framing**: We characterize distributional drift in recursive non-paraphrastic LLM generation, revealing **differential depth response**: level/diversity features (including reference-model entropy_mean) drift monotonically with depth, while distributional shape features (entropy variance, tail decay) remain near-stationary. This split is about **what the feature measures** (central tendency vs shape), not where it comes from. The combination of drifting and stationary features yields a 7-feature ensemble (LOGO F1=0.80 [0.786, 0.799]) that **significantly and consistently** outperforms all single-detector baselines (p<0.001 vs Avg-LogProb 0.77, LD-Score 0.72, FastDetectGPT 0.63, Binoculars 0.58).   "content": "[Reviewer 回复]:\n## 审查报告 — bootstrap-ci 分析\n\n**结论**: WARNING\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: warning] **新 bootstrap_ci_results.json 的 per-generator F1 与权威源 cross_generator_results.json (plan-014) 不一致**\n\n  6-gen LOGO 对比：\n\n  | Generator | plan-014 (权威) | 新 bootstrap | Δ |\n  |-----------|----------------|-------------|---|\n  | olmo-2-1b | 0.764 | 0.786 | +0.023 |\n  | pythia-1b | 0.856 | 0.828 | -0.028 |\n  | pythia-2.8b | 0.804 | 0.817 | +0.013 |\n  | qwen3-0.6b | 0.824 | 0.800 | -0.024 |\n  | qwen3-1.7b | 0.805 | 0.793 | -0.012 |\n  | qwen3-8b | 0.683 | 0.708 | +0.025 |\n  | **Average** | **0.789** | **0.788** | **-0.001** |\n\n  平均值仅差 0.1pp（因偏差正负相消），但 per-generator 偏差达 2.8pp。两个文件使用了不同的 LOGO 评估代码路径。cross_generator_results.json（plan-014 / cross_generator_eval.py）是论文引用的权威源。\n\n  类似地，5-gen LOGO：新 bootstrap=0.8034 vs plan-015-v2=0.798 vs plan-002-eval=0.8037。新 bootstrap 与 plan-002-eval 一致但与 plan-015-v2（论文当前引用源）不一致。\n\n- [severity: warning] **不建议用新 bootstrap 的 per-generator CI 更新论文**，除非先确认新脚本与 cross_generator_eval.py 的 LOGO 实现完全一致。当前两套数字来自不同代码路径，混用会引入不一致。\n\n- [severity: info] cross_generator_results.json 中**已包含 per-generator bootstrap CI**（n=1000），例如 pythia-1b CI [0.846, 0.866]、qwen3-8b CI [0.671, 0.696]。如果论文需要 6-gen CI，直接用这组即可，无需新脚本。\n\n- [severity: info] cross_generator_results.json 还包含 leave-one-feature-out 结果：去掉 entropy_std 后 LOGO 从 0.7893 降至 0.7853（Δ=0.004），验证了 entropy_std 边际贡献极小。这可以直接作为 feature ablation appendix 的内容。\n\n### 建议\n\n1. **论文数字不动**——继续使用 plan-014 的 cross_generator_results.json 作为权威源\n2. **如需 6-gen 整体 CI**：从 cross_generator_results.json 已有的 per-generator CI 计算即可（6 个 fold 的 bootstrap CI 已在文件中）\n3. **如需在论文中报告 6-gen 整体 CI**：可取 6 个 per-generator CI 的 envelope，或对整个 test set 做 bootstrap——但后者需要用与 cross_generator_eval.py 一致的代码\n4. 新 bootstrap 脚本需要排查与权威脚本的差异来源（可能是 XGBoost 初始化、数据排序、或 LOGO fold 实现不同），但这不是投稿阻塞项"

## 审查报告 — bootstrap-ci 分析

**结论**: WARNING

### 发现

- [severity: warning] **新 bootstrap_ci_results.json 的 per-generator F1 与权威源 cross_generator_results.json (plan-014) 不一致**

  6-gen LOGO 对比：

  | Generator | plan-014 (权威) | 新 bootstrap | Δ |
  |-----------|----------------|-------------|---|
  | olmo-2-1b | 0.764 | 0.786 | +0.023 |
  | pythia-1b | 0.856 | 0.828 | -0.028 |
  | pythia-2.8b | 0.804 | 0.817 | +0.013 |
  | qwen3-0.6b | 0.824 | 0.800 | -0.024 |
  | qwen3-1.7b | 0.805 | 0.793 | -0.012 |
  | qwen3-8b | 0.683 | 0.708 | +0.025 |
  | **Average** | **0.789** | **0.788** | **-0.001** |

  平均值仅差 0.1pp（因偏差正负相消），但 per-generator 偏差达 2.8pp。两个文件使用了不同的 LOGO 评估代码路径。cross_generator_results.json（plan-014 / cross_generator_eval.py）是论文引用的权威源。

  类似地，5-gen LOGO：新 bootstrap=0.8034 vs plan-015-v2=0.798 vs plan-002-eval=0.8037。新 bootstrap 与 plan-002-eval 一致但与 plan-015-v2（论文当前引用源）不一致。

- [severity: warning] **不建议用新 bootstrap 的 per-generator CI 更新论文**，除非先确认新脚本与 cross_generator_eval.py 的 LOGO 实现完全一致。当前两套数字来自不同代码路径，混用会引入不一致。

- [severity: info] cross_generator_results.json 中**已包含 per-generator bootstrap CI**（n=1000），例如 pythia-1b CI [0.846, 0.866]、qwen3-8b CI [0.671, 0.696]。如果论文需要 6-gen CI，直接用这组即可，无需新脚本。

- [severity: info] cross_generator_results.json 还包含 leave-one-feature-out 结果：去掉 entropy_std 后 LOGO 从 0.7893 降至 0.7853（Δ=0.004），验证了 entropy_std 边际贡献极小。这可以直接作为 feature ablation appendix 的内容。

### 建议

1. **论文数字不动**——继续使用 plan-014 的 cross_generator_results.json 作为权威源
2. **如需 6-gen 整体 CI**：从 cross_generator_results.json 已有的 per-generator CI 计算即可（6 个 fold 的 bootstrap CI 已在文件中）
3. **如需在论文中报告 6-gen 整体 CI**：可取 6 个 per-generator CI 的 envelope，或对整个 test set 做 bootstrap——但后者需要用与 cross_generator_eval.py 一致的代码
4. 新 bootstrap 脚本需要排查与权威脚本的差异来源（可能是 XGBoost 初始化、数据排序、或 LOGO fold 实现不同），但这不是投稿阻塞项

[Reviewer 回复]:
## 审查报告 — 5-class depth 分析

**结论**: PASS（数据有效，建议纳入论文 appendix）

### 发现

- [severity: info] **5-class LOGO 结果验证 3-class 设计决策合理**

  5-class（depth 0-4）LOGO per-generator macro-F1 范围 0.50-0.55，within-generator CV 均值 0.492±0.005。相比 3-class LOGO 0.789 下降约 30pp。

  Per-class 模式（以 olmo-2-1b fold 为代表）：
  - depth-0: F1=0.939（近完美，与 3-class 一致）
  - depth-1: F1=0.601（与 3-class gen-1 的 0.64 接近）
  - depth-2: F1=0.279（崩塌）
  - depth-3: F1=0.251（崩塌）
  - depth-4: F1=0.419（略好于 2-3，极端深度有弱信号）

  混淆矩阵显示 depths 2/3/4 互相严重混淆——例如 depth-2 的 947 样本中仅 225 正确，440 被误分为 depth-1。这定量验证了论文 Limitations 第 5 条中的假设："finer-grained may be feasible"应修正为有实证数据的表述。

- [severity: info] **5-class 特征重要性有趣变化**

  entropy_std 重要性从 3-class 的 0.046 升至 5-class 的 0.089（+93%），tail_decay 从 0.012 升至 0.060（+400%）。"stationary" shape features 在 5-class 中被更多依赖，尽管整体效果不佳。这说明当分类器试图区分 depths 2-4 时需要调用所有可用信号，但 shape features 无法提供足够的分辨率。

- [severity: info] **generalization gap = -0.0145**（within-gen CV 0.492 → LOGO ~0.478），比 3-class 的 gap 更大，说明 5-class 的跨生成器泛化更脆弱。

### 建议

1. **纳入 appendix**：这是直接回答 RC3（"Why only 3 classes?"）的实证数据。建议添加一张 appendix 表：5-class LOGO per-generator F1 + 代表性混淆矩阵 + 与 3-class 的对比
2. **更新 Limitations 第 5 条**：从"we do not evaluate this possibility"改为有数据支撑的表述，如"5-class evaluation yields LOGO F1 ≈ 0.50, with depths 2–4 mutually indistinguishable, validating the 3-class design"
3. 数据完整性确认：27,529 records、6 generators、depth 分布合理（递减 5972→4859），与 features_all_models.csv 一致 ✓

  "content": "[Reviewer 回复]:\n## 审查报告 — multi-seed-stability 分析\n\n**结论**: PASS（结果有效，建议纳入论文）\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: info] **XGBoost 种子稳定性极好**\n\n  5 个种子（42, 123, 456, 789, 1024）的 6-gen LOGO F1：\n  - mean = 0.7904, **std = 0.0013**\n  - range: [0.7886, 0.7922]，span 仅 0.36pp\n\n  这是强 reproducibility 证据——XGBoost 随机初始化对结果影响可忽略。建议在论文 method 或 appendix 中添加一句：\"LOGO F1 is stable across XGBoost random seeds (5 seeds, std = 0.001).\"\n\n- [severity: warning] **per-generator F1 与 plan-014 权威源的系统性偏差（与 bootstrap-ci 分析同一问题）**\n\n  | Generator | plan-014 (权威) | multi-seed 均值 | Δ |\n  |-----------|----------------|----------------|---|\n  | olmo-2-1b | 0.764 | 0.789 | +0.025 |\n  | pythia-1b | 0.856 | 0.830 | -0.026 |\n  | pythia-2.8b | 0.804 | 0.815 | +0.011 |\n  | qwen3-0.6b | 0.824 | 0.802 | -0.022 |\n  | qwen3-1.7b | 0.805 | 0.795 | -0.010 |\n  | qwen3-8b | 0.683 | 0.711 | +0.028 |\n  | **Average** | **0.789** | **0.790** | **+0.001** |\n\n  模式与 bootstrap-ci 分析完全一致：新脚本的 per-generator 值更\"压缩\"（高的变低、低的变高），平均值几乎相同。这确认了 **两套不同的 LOGO 实现** 在并行使用：\n  - plan-014 的 `cross_generator_eval.py`（论文当前引用源）\n  - 新分析脚本（bootstrap-ci + multi-seed 共用）\n\n  两者最大偏差在 Qwen3-8B（0.683 vs 0.711, Δ=2.8pp）和 Pythia-1B（0.856 vs 0.830, Δ=2.6pp）。对于 paper 中 \"per-generator range 0.68–0.86\" 的 claim，如果用新脚本的数字 range 变为 0.71–0.83，叙事会显著不同（gap 更小，但 headline 更保守）。\n\n  **根因猜测**：可能是 XGBoost `eval_metric`、class weighting、或 LOGO fold 中 stratification 方式不同。需要 Agent 对比两个脚本的 LOGO 实现。\n\n### 建议\n\n1. **种子稳定性结果值得纳入论文**——std=0.001 是有力的 reproducibility 证据\n2. **论文数字不动**——继续使用 plan-014 权威源。多种子均值 0.790 vs plan-014 的 0.789 差异可忽略\n3. **per-generator 偏差需要排查根因**——不紧急（不阻塞投稿，因为 headline 数字一致），但如果审稿人要求 reproducibility 说明，需要能解释两套实现的差异来源"

[Reviewer 回复]:
## 审查报告 — multi-seed-stability 分析

**结论**: PASS（结果有效，建议纳入论文）

### 发现

- [severity: info] **XGBoost 种子稳定性极好**

  5 个种子（42, 123, 456, 789, 1024）的 6-gen LOGO F1：
  - mean = 0.7904, **std = 0.0013**
  - range: [0.7886, 0.7922]，span 仅 0.36pp

  这是强 reproducibility 证据——XGBoost 随机初始化对结果影响可忽略。建议在论文 method 或 appendix 中添加一句："LOGO F1 is stable across XGBoost random seeds (5 seeds, std = 0.001)."

- [severity: warning] **per-generator F1 与 plan-014 权威源的系统性偏差（与 bootstrap-ci 分析同一问题）**

  | Generator | plan-014 (权威) | multi-seed 均值 | Δ |
  |-----------|----------------|----------------|---|
  | olmo-2-1b | 0.764 | 0.789 | +0.025 |
  | pythia-1b | 0.856 | 0.830 | -0.026 |
  | pythia-2.8b | 0.804 | 0.815 | +0.011 |
  | qwen3-0.6b | 0.824 | 0.802 | -0.022 |
  | qwen3-1.7b | 0.805 | 0.795 | -0.010 |
  | qwen3-8b | 0.683 | 0.711 | +0.028 |
  | **Average** | **0.789** | **0.790** | **+0.001** |

  模式与 bootstrap-ci 分析完全一致：新脚本的 per-generator 值更"压缩"（高的变低、低的变高），平均值几乎相同。这确认了 **两套不同的 LOGO 实现** 在并行使用：
  - plan-014 的 `cross_generator_eval.py`（论文当前引用源）
  - 新分析脚本（bootstrap-ci + multi-seed 共用）

  两者最大偏差在 Qwen3-8B（0.683 vs 0.711, Δ=2.8pp）和 Pythia-1B（0.856 vs 0.830, Δ=2.6pp）。对于 paper 中 "per-generator range 0.68–0.86" 的 claim，如果用新脚本的数字 range 变为 0.71–0.83，叙事会显著不同（gap 更小，但 headline 更保守）。

  **根因猜测**：可能是 XGBoost `eval_metric`、class weighting、或 LOGO fold 中 stratification 方式不同。需要 Agent 对比两个脚本的 LOGO 实现。

### 建议

1. **种子稳定性结果值得纳入论文**——std=0.001 是有力的 reproducibility 证据
2. **论文数字不动**——继续使用 plan-014 权威源。多种子均值 0.790 vs plan-014 的 0.789 差异可忽略
3. **per-generator 偏差需要排查根因**——不紧急（不阻塞投稿，因为 headline 数字一致），但如果审稿人要求 reproducibility 说明，需要能解释两套实现的差异来源

[Reviewer 回复]:
## 审查报告 — LOFO (Leave-One-Feature-Out) Ablation

**结论**: PASS（结果有效且有新发现，强烈建议纳入论文 appendix）

### 发现

- [severity: info] **LOFO 结果排序（按 F1 降幅）**

  | 去掉的特征 | LOGO F1 | Δ from full | XGBoost 重要性排名 |
  |-----------|---------|-------------|------------------|
  | entropy_max | 0.774 | **-1.50pp** | 4th (0.063) |
  | entropy_mean | 0.783 | -0.63pp | 2nd (0.164) |
  | ttr | 0.784 | -0.55pp | 3rd (0.159) |
  | hapax_ratio | 0.785 | -0.42pp | 1st (0.269) |
  | tail_decay | 0.786 | -0.33pp | 7th (0.012) |
  | entropy_std | 0.786 | -0.29pp | 5th (0.046) |
  | entropy_min | 0.789 | **+0.01pp** | 6th (0.015) |

  **全部 7 特征中 6 个有正向贡献（去掉后 F1 下降），1 个完全冗余（entropy_min）。**

- [severity: info] **关键发现：entropy_max 的"隐藏关键性"**

  entropy_max 在 XGBoost split importance 中排第 4（0.063），但 LOFO 影响排第 1（-1.5pp），是第 2 名的 2.4 倍。这意味着 entropy_max 的 split 频次低但提供了其他特征无法替代的独特信号。其 Spearman ρ = 0.095（近零）归类为 stationary，可能捕捉了罕见 token 出现频率的跨生成器校准信号。

  **这对论文叙事有价值**——LOFO 和 XGBoost importance 提供互补的特征重要性视角，可以作为 appendix 的一个分析亮点。

- [severity: info] **entropy_min 完全冗余（Δ ≈ 0）**

  去掉 entropy_min 后 F1 从 0.7904 升至 0.7891——实质无变化。与论文已有表述一致："Its low importance (0.015) makes the grouping boundary inconsequential." LOFO 提供了更强的定量佐证。

- [severity: info] **hapax_ratio vs ttr 的冗余性**

  hapax_ratio 有最高 XGBoost importance（0.269）但 LOFO 仅排第 4（-0.42pp），说明它的信号大量被 ttr 覆盖（两者同为词汇多样性度量）。这进一步支持论文的"complementary feature groups"叙事——同组内特征有冗余，但跨组（surface vs reference）互补。

- [severity: info] **实现一致性注记**

  Full baseline F1 = 0.7904（新脚本）vs plan-014 = 0.7893（权威源）。与之前报告的实现差异一致。LOFO 的相对排序（delta 方向和排名）不受此影响，可以直接使用。

### 建议

1. **强烈建议纳入 appendix**：添加 LOFO ablation 表（7 行），附简短分析。这直接回答"每个特征真的有用吗？"——审稿人的高频问题
2. **可在正文 feature ablation 段落添加一句**：如 "Leave-one-feature-out analysis confirms that 6 of 7 features contribute positively; entropy\_max shows the largest marginal impact despite modest split frequency (Appendix X)."
3. entropy_min 的完全冗余性可以在论文中更明确地陈述——当前只说 "low importance"，LOFO 数据允许更强的表述

    "snippet": "1\t\\section{Results}\n2\t\\label{sec:results}\n3\t\n4\tWe evaluate depth fingerprinting along four axes: cross-generator classification (\\S\\ref{sec:results-classification}), differential depth response of individual features (\\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}), comparison with existing detection baselines (\\S\\ref{sec:results-baselines}), and downstream utility for data curation (\\S\\ref{sec:results-downstream}).\n5\t\n6\t\\subsection{Depth Classification Performance}\n7\t\\label{sec:results-classification}\n8\t\n9\tTable~\\ref{tab:logo-results} reports leave-one-generator-out (LOGO) macro-F1 for 3-class depth estimation (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+) across six generators spanning three model families and parameter counts from 0.6B to 8.2B.\n10\t\n11\tThe 7-feature ensemble achieves LOGO F1\\,=\\,0.789, significantly outperforming all single-detector baselines ($p<0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:results-baselines}).\n12\tPer-generator held-out F1 ranges from 0.683 (Qwen3-8B) to 0.856 (Pythia-1B).\n13\tPer-class F1 varies by depth: gen-0\\,=\\,0.94, gen-2+\\,=\\,0.80, gen-1\\,=\\,0.64; the primary difficulty lies in distinguishing gen-1 from gen-2+, where feature margins narrow.\n14\t\n15\t\\paragraph{Cross-scale transfer.}\n16\tThe six-generator evaluation includes Qwen3-8B (8.2B parameters), confirming that depth signal persists at scales beyond the $\\leq$2.8B training generators.\n17\tHowever, cross-scale transfer incurs a moderate gap: Qwen3-8B held-out F1\\,=\\,0.683 (CI [0.672, 0.698]), compared to 0.76--0.86 for $\\leq$2.8B generators.\n18\tText generated by larger models is more fluent, compressing feature margins and making depth distinctions harder.\n19\tNotably, adding Qwen3-8B data \\emph{improves} classification on the original five generators (+1.1\\,pp to 0.811), indicating that cross-scale diversity benefits rather than dilutes the training signal.\n20\t\n21\t\\paragraph{Cross-family generalization.}\n22\tLeave-one-family-out (LOFO) evaluation---holding out an entire model family and training on the remaining two---yields macro-F1 of 0.772 (Pythia held out) and 0.761 (OLMo held out), confirming that depth features transfer across architectural families without degradation.\n23\t\n24\t\\paragraph{Length confound control.}\n25\tText length correlates weakly with depth (Spearman $|\\rho| < 0.14$).\n26\tLength-normalized feature trends are preserved, and length-matched subsampling yields comparable or higher F1 ($\\Delta$\\,=\\,+0.041), ruling out length as a confound (Appendix~\\ref{app:length}).\n27\t\n28\t\n29\t\\begin{table}[t]\n30\t\\centering\n31\t\\small\n32\t\\begin{tabular}{llccc}\n33\t\\toprule\n34\t\\textbf{Generator} & \\textbf{Family} & \\textbf{Params} & \\textbf{Held-out F1} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} \\\\\n35\t\\midrule\n36\tPythia-1B       & Pythia & 1.0B & 0.856 & [0.846, 0.866] \\\\\n37\tPythia-2.8B     & Pythia & 2.8B & 0.804 & [0.794, 0.814] \\\\\n38\tOLMo-2-1B      & OLMo   & 1.5B & 0.764 & [0.752, 0.775] \\\\\n39\tQwen3-0.6B     & Qwen   & 0.6B & 0.824 & [0.814, 0.835] \\\\\n40\tQwen3-1.7B     & Qwen   & 1.7B & 0.805 & [0.793, 0.815] \\\\\n41\tQwen3-8B       & Qwen   & 8.2B & 0.683 & [0.670, 0.697] \\\\\n42\t\\midrule\n43\t\\multicolumn{3}{l}{Average (6-gen LOGO)} & 0.789 & --- \\\\\n44\t\\multicolumn{3}{l}{5-gen LOGO (excl.\\ Qwen3-8B)} & 0.800 & --- \\\\\n45\t\\bottomrule\n46\t\\end{tabular}\n47\t\\caption{Leave-one-generator-out macro-F1 with bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals ($n$\\,=\\,1000) for 3-class depth estimation using the 7-feature ensemble. Each row holds out one generator for testing and trains on the remaining five.}\n48\t\\label{tab:logo-results}\n49\t\\end{table}\n50\t\n51\t\n52\t\\subsection{Differential Depth Response}\n53\t\\label{sec:results-differential}\n54\t\n55\tFeatures exhibit \\emph{differential depth response}: central-tendency and diversity measures drift monotonically with depth, while distributional shape measures remain near-stationary.\n56\tThis pattern is consistent with recursive generation inducing a location shift in the token-level NLL distribution---the text becomes progressively more predictable (mean shifts), while the functional form of the distribution is preserved (shape is invariant).\n57\t\n58\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n59\t\\centering\n60\t\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig1_differential_response.pdf}\n61\t\\caption{Differential depth response: Spearman correlations between individual features and generation depth. Central-tendency and diversity features (hapax ratio, TTR, entropy mean) show strong monotonic drift, while shape features (entropy std, tail decay) remain near-stationary.}\n62\t\\label{fig:feature-drift}\n63\t\\end{figure}\n64\t\n65\tIndividual-level Spearman correlations with depth confirm the split (Figure~\\ref{fig:feature-drift}):\n66\tdrifting features show strong monotonic trends (hapax ratio $\\rho = -0.558$, TTR $\\rho = -0.542$, entropy mean $\\rho = -0.475$),\n67\twhile shape features show weak or negligible correlations (entropy std $|\\rho| = 0.076$, tail decay $|\\rho| = 0.089$).\n68\t\n69\t\\paragraph{Feature complementarity.}\n70\tTable~\\ref{tab:ablation} reports ablation results.\n71\tThe full 7-feature set (F1\\,=\\,0.789) significantly outperforms both surface-only features (TTR + hapax; F1\\,=\\,0.729) and reference-only features (entropy statistics + tail decay; F1\\,=\\,0.745; McNemar $p \\approx 0$).\n72\tPermutation importance ranks hapax ratio highest (0.269), followed by entropy mean (0.164) and TTR (0.159).\n73\tSurface features capture vocabulary impoverishment; reference features capture distributional shift---neither subsumes the other.\n74\t\n75\t\\paragraph{Cross-family reference models.}\n76\tTo confirm that differential depth response is not an artifact of the Pythia-410M reference model, we compute features using three cross-family references: Pythia-410M, OLMo-2-1B, and Qwen3-0.6B.\n77\tAll three achieve LOGO F1 between 0.80 and 0.82, with consistent drift directions across features.\n78\tThe pattern generalizes across reference model architectures.\n79\t\n80\t\n81\t\\begin{table}[t]\n82\t\\centering\n83\t\\small\n84\t\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n85\t\\toprule\n86\t\\textbf{Feature set} & \\textbf{LOGO F1} & \\textbf{Top feature (importance)} \\\\\n87\t\\midrule\n88\tFull (7 features)     & 0.789 & hapax\\_ratio (0.269) \\\\\n89\tSurface only (2)      & 0.729 & hapax\\_ratio (0.320) \\\\\n90\tReference only (5)    & 0.745 & entropy\\_mean (0.218) \\\\\n91\t\\bottomrule\n92\t\\end{tabular}\n93\t\\caption{Feature ablation (6-gen LOGO). Full $>$ surface = reference confirms complementarity between surface diversity features and reference-model distributional features.}\n94\t\\label{tab:ablation}\n95\t\\end{table}\n96\t\n97\t\\paragraph{Leave-one-feature-out.}\n98\tTable~\\ref{tab:lofo} reports leave-one-feature-out (LOFO) ablation results, complementing the permutation importance analysis.\n99\tDropping entropy\\_max causes the largest degradation ($-$1.5\\,pp), despite its modest permutation importance (rank 4), suggesting it provides unique signal not redundant with other features.\n100\tConversely, entropy\\_min is fully redundant ($\\Delta$\\,=\\,+0.000).\n101\t\n102\t\\begin{table}[t]\n103\t\\centering\n104\t\\small\n105\t\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n106\t\\toprule\n107\t\\textbf{Dropped feature} & \\textbf{LOGO F1} & $\\boldsymbol{\\Delta}$ \\textbf{vs Full} \\\\\n108\t\\midrule\n109\tentropy\\_max    & 0.774 & $-$0.015 \\\\\n110\tentropy\\_mean   & 0.783 & $-$0.006 \\\\\n111\tttr             & 0.784 & $-$0.006 \\\\\n112\thapax\\_ratio    & 0.785 & $-$0.004 \\\\\n113\ttail\\_decay     & 0.786 & $-$0.003 \\\\\n114\tentropy\\_std    & 0.786 & $-$0.003 \\\\\n115\tentropy\\_min    & 0.789 & $+$0.000 \\\\\n116\t\\midrule\n117\tFull (7 features) & 0.789 & --- \\\\\n118\t\\bottomrule\n119\t\\end{tabular}\n120\t\\caption{Leave-one-feature-out ablation (6-gen LOGO). Each row drops one feature and re-evaluates with the LOGO protocol. Sorted by impact magnitude.}\n121\t\\label{tab:lofo}\n122\t\\end{table}\n123\t\n124\t\n125\t\\subsection{Comparison with Detection Baselines}\n126\t\\label{sec:results-baselines}\n127\t\n128\t\\begin{figure}[t]\n129\t\\centering\n130\t\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig3_baseline_comparison.png}\n131\t\\caption{LOGO macro-F1 with 95\\% bootstrap confidence intervals for each detection baseline and the 7-feature ensemble. Single-detector CIs span 18--26\\,pp; the ensemble CI spans 1.4\\,pp.}\n132\t\\label{fig:baseline-ci}\n133\t\\end{figure}\n134\t\n\n... [63 lines truncated] ..."

### 发现

- [severity: warning] **新 appendix 文件仅在 artifacts 目录，未进入论文 真源**

  两个新 appendix 已创建在 `artifacts/_project/paper/`：
  - `appendix_confusion_matrix.tex`（混淆矩阵）
  - `appendix_finer_depth.tex`（5-class 评估）

  但 `docs/paper/`（编译源目录）仍只有 `appendix_prompt_type.tex`。`main.tex` line 35 也只 `\input{appendix_prompt_type}`。**新 appendix 不会出现在编译后的 PDF 中。**

  需要：
  1. 复制到 `docs/paper/`
  2. 在 `main.tex` 添加 `\input{appendix_confusion_matrix}` 和 `\input{appendix_finer_depth}`
  3. 两个文件缺少 `\section` 头，需要包装（如 `\section{Confusion Matrix Analysis}` 和 `\section{5-Class Depth Classification}`）

- [severity: info] **内容质量核实**

  **appendix_confusion_matrix.tex**：
  - 数字与 confusion_matrix_results.json 逐项匹配 ✓（5937/35/0, 368/4437/1113, 464/3646/11529）
  - Row %：99.4/0.6/0.0, 6.2/75.0/18.8, 3.0/23.3/73.7 ✓
  - N=27,529 ✓
  - Caption 准确描述了 gen-1 误分类方向性 ✓

  **appendix_finer_depth.tex**：
  - 6-gen per-generator F1 与 JSON 一致 ✓
  - 平均 0.507：(0.498+0.552+0.527+0.506+0.519+0.437)/6 = 0.5065 ≈ 0.507 ✓
  - Per-class F1（gen-0=0.936, gen-1=0.635, gen-2=0.276, gen-3=0.261, gen-4=0.425）合理 ✓
  - Shape 特征重要性增幅 93%/400% 与 JSON 数据一致（entropy_std 0.046→0.089, tail_decay 0.012→0.060）✓
  - "reduces LOGO macro F1 from 0.789 to 0.507" 正确 ✓

  Worker 名含 "lofo" 但未生成 LOFO ablation appendix。以下数据已就绪但未写入论文：
  - LOFO 7-feature ablation 表（lofo_ablation_results.json）
  - Multi-seed stability（mean=0.789, std=0.0009）
  - 6-gen bootstrap CI（cross_generator_results.json 中已有 per-generator CI）

### 建议

1. **优先**：将两个 appendix 文件集成到 docs/paper/ + main.tex
2. **补充**：创建 LOFO ablation appendix（数据已有，只需写 LaTeX 表格）
3. **可选**：在 method 或 appendix 添加一句 seed stability（"LOGO F1 is stable across XGBoost seeds: 0.789 ± 0.001, $n$=5"）

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is a Director (PI) session for the `gen-depth-fingerprint` project, driving toward EMNLP 2026 ARR submission (deadline May 25, 2026, 10 days away). The Director manages experiments, approves plans, steers research direction, and coordinates Paper Agent for paper writing. All communication to user goes through `director_reply_to_user`, to Agent via `director_post_to_main_agent`, to Paper Agent via `director_post_to_paper_agent`. All replies must be in Chinese per CLAUDE.md.

   The user (孙震) previously explicitly rejected switching venues: "实验可以做完，不允许换会议" — EMNLP is mandatory. All experiments are complete; the focus is paper quality maximization and submission preparation.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **Ordinal Depth Estimation**: 3-class classification (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+) using 7 distributional features
   - **Differential Depth Response**: Central-tendency/diversity features drift with depth; shape features near-stationary
   - **6-gen LOGO**: Leave-One-Generator-Out evaluation across 6 generators, 3 families (Pythia, OLMo, Qwen), F1=0.789
   - **LOFO**: Leave-One-Family-Out, bidirectional (Pythia held out: 0.772, OLMo held out: 0.761)
   - **LOPTO**: Leave-One-Prompt-Type-Out, avg F1=0.753
   - **Feature Complementarity**: Full 7-feature (0.789) > surface-only (0.729) > reference-only (0.745)
   - **Leave-One-Feature-Out (LOFO ablation)**: entropy_max most impactful (-1.5pp), entropy_min redundant (Δ≈0)
   - **5-class evaluation**: F1≈0.50, depths 2-4 indistinguishable, validates 3-class design
   - **Multi-seed stability**: mean=0.789, std=0.0009 across 5 XGBoost seeds
   - **Confusion matrix**: gen-1 errors 3:1 biased toward gen-2+ (18.8%) vs gen-0 (6.2%)
   - **Negative downstream finding**: Depth-aware curation doesn't beat random subsampling (PPL 2.387 vs 2.367)
   - **Two LOGO implementations detected**: New analysis scripts had per-generator F1 diverging from plan-014 by up to 2.8pp; root cause was implementation difference in LOGO fold/stratification. Corrected in v2 (Δ≤0.5pp). Decision: use plan-014's cross_generator_results.json as authoritative source.

3. Files and Code Sections:

macro-F1 for 3-class depth estimation (gen-0\\,/\\,gen-1\\,/\\,gen-2+) across six generators spanning three model families and parameter counts from 0.6B to 8.2B.\n\nThe 7-feature ensemble achieves LOGO F1\\,=\\,0.789, significantly outperforming all single-detector baselines ($p<0.001$; \\S\\ref{sec:results-baselines}).\nPer-generator held-out F1 ranges from 0.683 (Qwen3-8B) to 0.856 (Pythia-1B).\nPer-class F1 varies by depth: gen-0\\,=\\,0.94, gen-2+\\,=\\,0.80, gen-1\\,=\\,0.64; the primary difficulty lies in distinguishing gen-1 from gen-2+, where feature margins narrow.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-scale transfer.}\nThe six-generator evaluation includes Qwen3-8B (8.2B parameters), confirming that depth signal persists at scales beyond the $\\leq$2.8B training generators.\nHowever, cross-scale transfer incurs a moderate gap: Qwen3-8B held-out F1\\,=\\,0.683 (CI [0.672, 0.698]), compared to 0.76--0.86 for $\\leq$2.8B generators.\nText generated by larger models is more fluent, compressing feature margins and making depth distinctions harder.\nNotably, adding Qwen3-8B data \\emph{improves} classification on the original five generators (+1.1\\,pp to 0.811), indicating that cross-scale diversity benefits rather than dilutes the training signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-family generalization.}\nLeave-one-family-out (LOFO) evaluation---holding out an entire model family and training on the remaining two---yields macro-F1 of 0.772 (Pythia held out) and 0.761 (OLMo held out), confirming that depth features transfer across architectural families without degradation.\n\n\\paragraph{Length confound control.}\nText length correlates weakly with depth (Spearman $|\\rho| < 0.14$).\nLength-normalized feature trends are preserved, and length-matched subsampling yields comparable or higher F1 ($\\Delta$\\,=\\,+0.041), ruling out length as a confound (Appendix~\\ref{app:length}).\n\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{llccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Generator} & \\textbf{Family} & \\textbf{Params} & \\textbf{Held-out F1} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia-1B       & Pythia & 1.0B & 0.856 & [0.846, 0.866] \\\\\nPythia-2.8B     & Pythia & 2.8B & 0.804 & [0.794, 0.814] \\\\\nOLMo-2-1B      & OLMo   & 1.5B & 0.764 & [0.752, 0.775] \\\\\nQwen3-0.6B     & Qwen   & 0.6B & 0.824 & [0.814, 0.835] \\\\\nQwen3-1.7B     & Qwen   & 1.7B & 0.805 & [0.793, 0.815] \\\\\nQwen3-8B       & Qwen   & 8.2B & 0.683 & [0.670, 0.697] \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{3}{l}{Average (6-gen LOGO)} & 0.789 & --- \\\\\n\\multicolumn{3}{l}{5-gen LOGO (excl.\\ Qwen3-8B)} & 0.800 & --- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Leave-one-generator-out macro-F1 with bootstrap 95\\% confidence intervals ($n$\\,=\\,1000) for 3-class depth estimation using the 7-feature ensemble. Each row holds out one generator for testing and trains on the remaining five.}\n\\label{tab:logo-results}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Differential Depth Response}\n\\label{sec:results-differential}\n\nFeatures exhibit \\emph{differential depth response}: central-tendency and diversity measures drift monotonically with depth, while distributional shape measures remain near-stationary.\nThis pattern is consistent with recursive generation inducing a location shift in the token-level NLL distribution---the text becomes progressively more predictable (mean shifts), while the functional form of the distribution is preserved (shape is invariant).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig1_differential_response.pdf}\n\\caption{Differential depth response: Spearman correlations between individual features and generation depth. Central-tendency and diversity features (hapax ratio, TTR, entropy mean) show strong monotonic drift, while shape features (entropy std, tail decay) remain near-stationary.}\n\\label{fig:feature-drift}\n\\end{figure}\n\nIndividual-level Spearman correlations with depth confirm the split (Figure~\\ref{fig:feature-drift}):\ndrifting features show strong monotonic trends (hapax ratio $\\rho = -0.558$, TTR $\\rho = -0.542$, entropy mean $\\rho = -0.475$),\nwhile shape features show weak or negligible correlations (entropy std $|\\rho| = 0.076$, tail decay $|\\rho| = 0.089$).\n\n\\paragraph{Feature complementarity.}\nTable~\\ref{tab:ablation} reports ablation results.\nThe full 7-feature set (F1\\,=\\,0.789) significantly outperforms both surface-only features (TTR + hapax; F1\\,=\\,0.729) and reference-only features (entropy statistics + tail decay; F1\\,=\\,0.745; McNemar $p \\approx 0$).\nPermutation importance ranks hapax ratio highest (0.269), followed by entropy mean (0.164) and TTR (0.159).\nSurface features capture vocabulary impoverishment; reference features capture distributional shift---neither subsumes the other.\n\n\\paragraph{Cross-family reference models.}\nTo confirm that differential depth response is not an artifact of the Pythia-410M reference model, we compute features using three cross-family references: Pythia-410M, OLMo-2-1B, and Qwen3-0.6B.\nAll three achieve LOGO F1 between 0.80 and 0.82, with consistent drift directions across features.\nThe pattern generalizes across reference model architectures.\n\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Feature set} & \\textbf{LOGO F1} & \\textbf{Top feature (importance)} \\\\\n\\midrule\nFull (7 features)     & 0.789 & hapax\\_ratio (0.269) \\\\\nSurface only (2)      & 0.729 & hapax\\_ratio (0.320) \\\\\nReference only (5)    & 0.745 & entropy\\_mean (0.218) \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Feature ablation (6-gen LOGO). Full $>$ surface = reference confirms complementarity between surface diversity features and reference-model distributional features.}\n\\label{tab:ablation}\n\\end{table}\n\nLeave-one-feature-out analysis confirms that 6 of 7 features contribute positively, with entropy\\_max showing the largest marginal impact despite modest split frequency (Appendix~\\ref{app:lofo}).\n\n\n\\subsection{Comparison with Detection Baselines}\n\\label{sec:results-baselines}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{fig3_baseline_comparison.png}\n\\caption{LOGO macro-F1 with 95\\% bootstrap confidence intervals for each detection baseline and the 7-feature ensemble. Single-detector CIs span 18--26\\,pp; the ensemble CI spans 1.4\\,pp.}\n\\label{fig:baseline-ci}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe repurpose four existing detection scores as ordinal depth predictors by training the same ordinal classifier on each score as a single feature, using identical LOGO evaluation (Figure~\\ref{fig:baseline-ci}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lcc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Method} & \\textbf{LOGO F1} & \\textbf{95\\% CI} \\\\\n\\midrule\n7-feature ensemble & \\textbf{0.800} & [0.786, 0.800] \\\\\nAvg-LogProb        & 0.765 & [0.616, 0.820] \\\\\nLD-Score            & 0.721 & [0.588, 0.770] \\\\\nFastDetectGPT       & 0.627 & [0.511, 0.762] \\\\\nBinoculars          & 0.579 & [0.405, 0.664] \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Ordinal depth classification: 7-feature ensemble vs.\\ single detection baselines (5-gen LOGO). The ensemble outperforms the best single detector (Avg-LogProb) by +3.5\\,pp (paired bootstrap $p < 0.001$, $\\Delta$ CI [3.0, 4.0]).}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe ensemble outperforms all baselines in mean F1: +3.5\\,pp over Avg-LogProb ($p < 0.001$, $\\Delta$ CI [3.0, 4.0]), +7.9\\,pp over LD-Score, +17.3\\,pp over FastDetectGPT, and +22.1\\,pp over Binoculars (Table~\\ref{tab:baselines}).\n\nThe critical distinction is \\emph{reliability}.\nThe ensemble CI spans just 1.4\\,pp [0.786, 0.800], while single-detector CIs span 18--26\\,pp (e.g., Avg-LogProb [0.616, 0.820]).\nA single detector's CI is wider than \\emph{all pairwise detector differences}, meaning no single detector is reliably best across generators.\nEach detector captures partial depth signal---Avg-LogProb tracks mean perplexity shift, LD-Score captures character-level distributional change---but each fails on specific generator families.\nThe 7-feature ensemble aggregates complementary signals, producing stable performance regardless of the held-out generator.\n\n\n\\subsection{Downstream Utility: A Negative Finding}\n\\label{sec:results-downstream}\n\nCan depth-aware data curation improve downstream language model quality?\nWe fine-tune OLMo-2-0425-1B on five data variants (3 seeds each) and evaluate perplexity on a held-out test set (Table~\\ref{tab:downstream}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{lrc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Variant} & \\textbf{$n$} & \\textbf{Test PPL} \\\\\n\\midrule\nAll depths            & 12{,}299 & 2.335 $\\pm$ 0.000 \\\\\nSize-matched (random) & 3{,}951  & 2.367 $\\pm$ 0.000 \\\\\nDepth 0--1 only       & 3{,}951  & 2.387 $\\pm$ 0.001 \\\\\nBinary-filtered       & 3{,}951  & 2.389 $\\pm$ 0.000 \\\\\nDepth 0 only          & 797      & 2.681 $\\pm$ 0.001 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\caption{Downstream fine-tuning perplexity (OLMo-2-0425-1B, mean $\\pm$ std across 3 seeds). At equal sample count, random subsampling (2.367) outperforms depth-aware curation (depth 0--1: 2.387, binary-filtered: 2.389).}\n\\label{tab:downstream}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe results are unambiguous: data quantity dominates depth composition.\nTraining on all 12{,}299 samples yields the lowest perplexity (2.335).\nAt equal sample count ($n$\\,=\\,3{,}951), random subsampling (2.367) \\emph{outperforms} both depth-stratified selection (depth 0--1: 2.387) and binary detection filtering (2.389).\nCross-seed variance is below 0.002 across all variants, confirming the robustness of this null result.\n\nRestricting training to human-only text (depth 0, $n$\\,=\\,797) produces a sharp perplexity increase to 2.681, driven primarily by the 5$\\times$ reduction in data volume rather than depth composition per se.\n\nWe report this negative finding transparently: depth fingerprinting is an effective \\emph{diagnostic} tool for characterizing text provenance, not a \\emph{curative} tool for data curation.\nKnowing the generation depth of a document does not, by itself, make it more or less useful for training.\n",
        "numLines": 172,

Template\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\n\\graphicspath{{../chart/}}\n\n\\title{Depth Fingerprinting: Ordinal Generation Depth Estimation\\\\in Non-Paraphrastic LLM Text Chains}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nAs large language model (LLM) outputs accumulate on the web, training corpora increasingly contain recursively generated text of unknown provenance depth.\nWe formulate \\emph{ordinal depth estimation}---classifying whether text is human-written (gen-0), first-generation synthetic (gen-1), or deeper (gen-2+)---a task that moves beyond binary real-versus-synthetic detection.\nExisting detectors provide only binary scores and, when repurposed for ordinal prediction, exhibit high cross-generator variance (confidence intervals spanning 0.41--0.82), making them unreliable depth estimators.\nWe characterize a \\emph{differential depth response} in distributional features computed under a held-out reference model: central-tendency and diversity features (entropy mean, type-token ratio, hapax ratio) drift monotonically with generation depth, while distributional shape features (entropy standard deviation, tail decay rate) remain near-stationary.\nCombining both feature types into a 7-feature ensemble exploits this complementarity: the full set (macro-F1\\,=\\,0.79) outperforms surface-only (0.73) and reference-only (0.75) subsets.\nIn leave-one-generator-out evaluation across 6 generators from 3 model families (0.6B--8B parameters), the ensemble achieves macro-F1 of 0.79 ($p < 0.001$ vs.\\ all single-detector baselines), with per-generator held-out F1 ranging from 0.68 to 0.86.\nHowever, depth-aware data curation does not improve downstream language modeling quality compared to random subsampling---data quantity, not depth composition, is the primary determinant---positioning depth fingerprinting as a diagnostic tool for provenance characterization, not a curative tool for data selection.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{results}\n\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\\input{limitations}\n\\input{ethics}\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\section{Length Confound Analysis}\n\\label{app:length}\n\\input{appendix_length}\n\n\\section{Prompt Type Generalization}\n\\label{app:prompt-type}\n\\input{appendix_prompt_type}\n\n\\section{Finer-Grained Depth Classification}\n\\label{app:finer-depth}\n\\input{appendix_finer_depth}\n\n\\section{Leave-One-Feature-Out Analysis}\n\\label{app:lofo}\n\\input{appendix_lofo}\n\n\\section{Confusion Matrix}\n\\label{app:confusion}\n\\input{appendix_confusion_matrix}\n\n\\end{document}\n",

{
  "parentUuid": "5ae678c9-d95e-44d5-ad53-22b1c031de36",

        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/paper_framing.md",
        "content": "# Paper Framing — gen-depth-fingerprint\n\n## Target Venue\n- Primary: AAAI 2027 (deadline: August 2, 2026)\n- **Primary: EMNLP 2026 ARR (deadline: May 25, 2026)** — D012: 恢复为主投稿目标\n- Fallback venues: ICLR 2027 (Sep 24), NAACL 2027 ARR (Oct 16)\n\n## Core Insight\nNon-paraphrastic LLM generation chains exhibit **differential depth response**: **central-tendency/diversity features** (entropy_mean, TTR, hapax ratio) drift monotonically with depth, while **distributional shape features** (entropy_std, tail_decay) remain near-stationary. This differential response is consistent with recursive generation inducing a **location shift** in the NLL distribution — the text becomes progressively more predictable (mean shifts) while the functional form of the distribution is preserved (shape is invariant). The genuine novelty is not the drift/stationarity split per se (which follows from location-shift properties), but: (a) the **task formulation** (ordinal depth estimation beyond binary detection), (b) **empirical validation** across 6 generators / 3 model families (including 7B+ scale) showing this pattern is robust, (c) the practical consequence that combining both feature types yields **complementary signal** for depth classification (macro-F1 0.79 cross-generator with 6 generators, 0.77 cross-family). Depth signal is scale-invariant as a phenomenon, though cross-scale classifier transfer shows a moderate gap (8B held-out F1=0.69 vs ≤2.8B 0.76-0.85).\n\n## Core Claims (3 条，均可证伪)\n\n**Claim 1 — Depth Discriminability**: Distributional features computed under a held-out reference model can classify text generation depth (gen-0 / gen-1 / gen-2+) with macro-F1 significantly above chance, generalizing across unseen generators (leave-one-generator-out evaluation).\n\n**Claim 2 — Differential Depth Response & Feature Complementarity**: Depth-dependent features exhibit differential response: central-tendency/diversity features (entropy_mean, TTR, hapax_ratio) drift monotonically with depth (consistent with location shift in NLL distribution), while shape features (entropy_std, tail_decay) remain near-stationary. Full feature set (cross-gen 0.80) significantly outperforms surface-only (0.75) and reference-only (0.75) — the two feature groups are complementary. Notably, reference features generalize better across generators while surface features are stronger in-distribution — demonstrating complementarity across different evaluation axes.\n\n**Claim 3 — Downstream Utility (Negative Finding)**: Depth-aware data curation does NOT improve downstream CLM quality compared to random subsampling. At equal data count, size_matched (random control, ppl=2.367) outperforms depth01 (depth-stratified, 2.387) and binary_filtered (2.389). The primary determinant is data quantity, not depth composition (all 12K samples → ppl=2.335). Depth fingerprinting is an effective **diagnostic** tool for characterizing provenance, but not a **curative** tool for data curation. This honest negative finding is reported transparently.\n\n## Story Spine\n\n**Problem**: Web-scraped training data increasingly contains LLM-generated text of unknown provenance depth. Current detection methods offer only binary real-vs-synthetic classification, forcing all-or-nothing filtering: discard everything flagged as synthetic (losing useful gen-1 content) or keep everything (admitting high-risk deep-generation text with compounding distributional drift).\n\n**Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (FastDetectGPT, SynthID-Text, LD-Score) cannot distinguish gen-1 from gen-2+. Attractor cycle theory (ACL 2025) proves paraphrasing chains converge to 2-period cycles, bounding depth signal to ~3 classes — but this result depends on the semantic-preservation constraint of paraphrasing. No existing work attempts generation depth estimation. When repurposed as ordinal depth predictors, single detector scores (Avg-LogProb 0.77, LD-Score 0.72) show moderate signal but **high cross-generator variance** (CIs spanning 0.41–0.82), making them unreliable as standalone depth estimators.\n\n**Our framing**: We characterize distributional drift in recursive non-paraphrastic LLM generation, revealing **differential depth response**: level/diversity features (including reference-model entropy_mean) drift monotonically with depth, while distributional shape features (entropy variance, tail decay) remain near-stationary. This split is about **what the feature measures** (central tendency vs shape), not where it comes from. The combination of drifting and stationary features yields a 7-feature ensemble (LOGO F1=0.80 [0.786, 0.799]) that **significantly and consistently** outperforms all single-detector baselines (p<0.001 vs Avg-LogProb 0.77, LD-Score 0.72, FastDetectGPT 0.63, Binoculars 0.58). Crucially, single detectors exhibit high cross-generator variance (CIs spanning 0.41–0.82), while our ensemble maintains tight consistency — the practical advantage is **reliability across unknown generators**, not just average performance.\n\n**Headline result template**: \"On multi-generator continuation chains across 6 generators from 3 model families (0.6B–8.2B), our 7-feature distributional ensemble achieves 6-gen LOGO macro-F1 of 0.79 for 3-class depth estimation (gen-0/gen-1/gen-2+), with per-generator held-out F1 ranging from 0.68 to 0.86. Depth-aware data curation does not outperform random subsampling for downstream CLM quality, establishing depth fingerprinting as a diagnostic tool for provenance characterization, not a curative tool for data selection.\"\n\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\n\n**RC1 — \"Is this just vocabulary impoverishment?\"**: Surface features alone achieve 0.73 macro-F1, but full features achieve 0.79 (p<0.001). We don't claim reference features are \"better\" — we show they capture a **different, complementary** signal (stationary distributional shape vs drifting level/diversity). The dual dynamics finding is itself a scientific contribution. Note: \"fingerprint\" in the paper refers to the full feature vector's combination, not just reference-model features.\n\n**RC7 — Pre-registered prediction (LD-Score)**: LD-Score (character-distribution signature) measures character-level distributional properties. We predict it is a **level feature** (sensitive to vocabulary/character frequency shifts) and should exhibit **H₂ drift** with depth. This prediction is registered before plan_011 execution as an out-of-sample test of the level/shape framework.\n\n**RC2 — \"Does this generalize beyond Pythia?\"**: MVP used same-family models (Pythia-2.8B generator + Pythia-410M reference). → Cross-family experiments: Qwen/OLMo generators + non-Pythia reference models (RQ1 cross-generator evaluation).\n\n**RC3 — \"Is 3-class sufficient? Why not finer-grained?\"**: Attractor theory bounds paraphrasing to ~3 classes. → Secondary analysis: finer-grained ordinal classification (gen-0 through gen-4) on non-paraphrastic chains to test if more depth signal exists.\n\n**RC4 — \"How realistic are single-model chains?\"**: Real web contamination involves diverse generators. → Cross-model chains (different generator per depth step) as primary evaluation setting (RQ3).\n\n**RC5 — \"What about human editing / post-processing?\"**: Real-world synthetic text is often edited. → Robustness test with simulated human edits (random word replacement as lower-bound + LLM-simulated editing).\n\n**RC6 — \"Ecological validity — continuation vs real web content\"**: Lab-constructed chains may not match wild distribution. → Multi-domain seeds (news, Wikipedia, scientific, creative) + multiple prompt types (continuation, elaboration, inspired rewriting).\n\n**RC8 — \"Aren't these just OOD detection features?\"**: Reference-model entropy statistics are functionally NLL-based OOD scores (same signal backbone as DetectGPT/GLTR/Binoculars). Our contribution is NOT the features themselves but: (a) the problem formulation (ordinal depth estimation, not binary detection), (b) the empirical discovery that these features exhibit dual dynamics (level drift vs shape stationarity) under recursive generation, and (c) the practical application to risk-stratified data curation. Paper must explicitly connect to OOD detection + perplexity-based detection literature and position the contribution as primarily empirical/conceptual rather than methodological.\n\n**RC9 — \"Only ≤2.8B generators — does this transfer to frontier scale?\"**: **Answered (plan-014, D011).** Qwen3-8B (7.6B params) added as 6th generator. Depth signal IS present at 8B scale — all feature-depth Spearman correlations significant (hapax ρ=-0.568, ttr ρ=-0.553, entropy_mean ρ=-0.475, all p<1e-6), confirming depth fingerprinting is a scale-invariant phenomenon. However, cross-scale transfer shows a gap: Qwen3-8B held-out F1=0.69 vs ≤2.8B generators 0.76-0.85 (6-gen LOGO average 0.7886, -1.1pp from 5-gen 0.80). Notably, adding 8B data improves original 5-gen classification (+0.9pp to 0.8086). **Paper framing**: depth signal universality is supported (Claim 1 holds); cross-scale classifier transfer gap reported as honest limitation with analysis (8B text is more fluent → smaller feature margins → harder to distinguish from human text). The 6-gen LOGO 0.79 still significantly outperforms all single-detector baselines.\n\n**RC10 — \"Text length confound\"**: Hapax ratio and TTR are mechanically length-dependent. If generation chains produce texts of systematically different lengths, surface features may track length artifacts rather than distributional drift. → Length distribution analysis + length-normalized features + length-matched comparison (plan_013). Critical confound control.\n\n**RC11 — \"Downstream utility — single run, no CIs\"**: Depth-aware filtering advantage must be validated with multiple seeds + bootstrap CIs + sample-count-matched comparison (to control for data size confound). Must use non-Pythia model for downstream fine-tuning (OLMo-1B) to avoid reference model circularity (Pythia-410M as both feature extractor and eval model).\n\n## Contribution Positioning (D006+D007)\nThe contribution is **primarily empirical and conceptual**, not methodological:\n1. **Problem formulation**: Ordinal depth estimation as a new task beyond binary detection (genuinely novel — no prior work)\n2. **Empirical characterization**: Differential depth response across 6 generators / 3 families (including 7B+ scale), with robust cross-generator generalization (6-gen LOGO 0.79, family-out 0.77). Scale-invariant phenomenon with honest cross-scale transfer gap.\n3. **Negative finding**: Depth-aware data curation does NOT improve downstream CLM quality — depth fingerprinting is diagnostic, not curative (D009).\n\n**Framing discipline (D007 — re-dream consensus):**\n- Do NOT call the drift/stationarity split a \"discovery\" — it follows from location-shift properties of the NLL distribution (means shift under distributional drift, shapes don't). Acknowledge this explicitly.\n- Do NOT use H1/H2 terminology (overloads time-series unit-root convention). Use \"drifting\" vs \"stationary\" or \"differential response.\"\n- Do NOT overclaim \"dual dynamics\" as a novel theoretical framework. Use \"differential depth response\" — more calibrated.\n- Consider renaming \"distributional fingerprinting\" → \"distributional depth profiling\" (fingerprinting implies source identification, creates confusion with LLM attribution literature).\n- Provide at least a speculative theoretical motivation for WHY shape features remain stationary (e.g., \"shape is determined by sampling mechanism and architecture, invariant to input content drift\").\n- Report **realistic performance range** (0.69–0.85 per-generator held-out F1, 0.79 LOGO average across 6 generators) in abstract/conclusion, not just best-case.\n- R² on 5 aggregated depth means is statistically meaningless (3 df). Use individual-level Spearman ρ with bootstrap CIs throughout.\n\nRelated work must expand to 30+ references covering: perplexity-based detection (DetectGPT, GLTR, DNA-GPT, Binoculars), model collapse theory (Alemohammad et al., Dohmatob et al., Gerstgrasser et al., Guo et al.), OOD detection (Hendrycks & Gimpel), data quality scoring (DSIR, QuRating, CCNet), watermarking (Kirchenbauer et al.), vocabulary diversity (Guo et al. 2024, Padmakumar & He 2024, Holtzman et al. 2020), **stylometry/authorship attribution** (Stamatatos 2009, Koppel et al. 2009 — feature set is essentially stylometric), **iterative generation** (LLM as Broken Telephone ACL 2025, Markovian Generation Chains), **membership inference** (Shi et al. 2024).\n\n## Venue Fit\n- AAAI 2027: Fit as empirical + conceptual contribution — novel task formulation (depth estimation) + surprising empirical finding (dual dynamics) + practical application (data curation). Must position as empirical science, not methodology paper.\n- EMNLP 2026: Excellent fit — directly addresses special theme \"New Missions for NLP Research\" (data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance). NLP-native venue for text-focused work. Empirical/conceptual contributions better received here than AAAI.\n",
        "numLines": 73,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 73
      }
    },

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/conclusion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Conclusion}\n\\label{sec:conclusion}\n\nWe introduced ordinal generation depth estimation as a task beyond binary synthetic text detection, providing a framework for characterizing the provenance of web-crawled text at finer granularity.\nAcross six generators from three model families (0.6B--8.2B parameters), we documented differential depth response: central-tendency and diversity features drift monotonically with generation depth, while distributional shape features remain near-stationary.\nA 7-feature ensemble combining both feature types achieves leave-one-generator-out macro-F1 of 0.79, with per-generator held-out F1 ranging from 0.68 to 0.86.\nWe also reported that depth-aware data curation does not improve downstream language model quality over random subsampling, establishing depth fingerprinting as a diagnostic rather than curative tool.\n\nFuture work should extend evaluation to frontier-scale generators (70B+), mixed-generator chains that better approximate real-world contamination, and multilingual settings.\n",
        "numLines": 10,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 10
      }
    },

{
  "parentUuid": "48914a7c-e39b-4b3a-b489-ee9398b57820",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",
    "filename": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/discussion.tex",
    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_3/projects/gen-depth-fingerprint/artifacts/_project/paper/discussion.tex",
        "content": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Why are shape features stationary?}\nDistributional shape features (entropy standard deviation, tail decay) remain near-stationary across generation depths, while central-tendency features drift monotonically (\\S\\ref{sec:results-differential}).\nOne \\emph{speculative} explanation is that shape is primarily determined by the sampling mechanism (temperature, top-$p$ truncation) and the model's architecture-level output distribution, both of which are fixed across generation steps.\nRecursive generation shifts \\emph{what} the model talks about---steering toward higher-probability vocabulary regions---but does not alter \\emph{how} the model distributes probability mass around its predictions.\nIf this account is correct, shape features should remain stationary even at much greater depths and across different prompt types, while level features should continue to drift until reaching a fixed point.\nWe flag this as a hypothesis for future investigation, not a conclusion supported by our current experiments.\n\n\\paragraph{Relation to OOD detection.}\nReference-model entropy features are functionally NLL-based out-of-distribution scores---the same signal backbone underlying DetectGPT \\cite{detectgpt}, GLTR \\cite{gltr}, and Binoculars \\cite{binoculars}.\nOur contribution is not these features per se, but the \\emph{ordinal} depth formulation and the empirical finding that they exhibit differential depth response under recursive generation.\nThis connection also offers a lens on shape stationarity: in the OOD detection literature, distributional location statistics (mean log-likelihood) shift under domain drift, while shape statistics (variance, tail behavior) are more robust \\cite{ood_baseline}.\nRecursive generation chains produce an analogous effect---progressive drift away from human text that manifests primarily as a location shift in the NLL distribution.\n\n\\paragraph{Practical implications.}\nThe differential depth response enables risk-stratified data curation.\nRather than binary keep-or-discard filtering, practitioners can flag gen-2+ text for manual review while retaining gen-1 content that preserves much of the source distribution's properties.\nThree-class granularity is well-suited to this use case: it separates human text from moderate-drift first-generation content and from deeper text with compounding distributional shift---the distinction most relevant to curation decisions.\nFiner-grained depth distinctions may be achievable for non-paraphrastic chains (unlike paraphrastic chains, which converge within $\\sim$3 steps \\cite{broken_telephone}), but the three-class scheme already captures the practically relevant boundaries.\nThe tight cross-generator consistency of the ensemble (CI width 1.4\\,pp) makes deployment feasible even when the generating model is unknown.\n\n\\paragraph{Information value of the negative finding.}\nDepth-aware curation does not improve downstream language model quality over random subsampling at equal sample count (\\S\\ref{sec:results-downstream}).\nThis negative result carries positive information value: it rules out depth-based filtering as a data selection strategy, sparing practitioners from building depth-conditional pipelines that would not yield quality gains.\nThe dominant factor is data quantity, not depth composition---consistent with findings emphasizing data volume over curation sophistication \\cite{self_consuming_mad}.\n\n\\paragraph{Connection to model collapse.}\nOur results relate to, but are distinct from, model collapse \\cite{curse_of_recursion,strong_model_collapse,self_consuming_mad}.\nModel collapse describes distributional degradation when models are \\emph{retrained} on their own outputs across generations.\nWe observe analogous drift---monotonic decrease in vocabulary diversity, compression of perplexity---in \\emph{inference chains} without any parameter updates.\nThis suggests that the mechanisms documented in model collapse theory (regression toward high-probability modes, loss of distributional tails) operate at inference time as well, driven by the sampling process rather than gradient updates.\nThe shared phenomenology motivates using depth fingerprinting features as early indicators of data that, if fed back into training, may accelerate model collapse.\n",
        "numLines": 35,
        "startLine": 1,
        "totalLines": 35
      }
    },
    "displayPath": "../../../artifacts/_project/paper/discussion.tex"
  },

19:For each generator, we sample approximately 1{,}000 seed passages from English Wikipedia as $\mathcal{C}_0$.
20:Each seed is iteratively fed through the generator to produce a chain of depth 4 (i.e., gen-0 through gen-4), using the prompt ``\texttt{Continue the following text: \{text\}}''.
64:LOGO F1 is stable across random seeds (mean\,=\,0.789, std\,=\,0.0009 over 5 seeds), confirming that results are not sensitive to classifier initialization.

1	% EMNLP 2026 ARR Submission — ACL 2023 Template
2	\documentclass[11pt]{article}

13	\title{Depth Fingerprinting: Ordinal Generation Depth Estimation\\in Non-Paraphrastic LLM Text Chains}
14	
15	\author{Anonymous}
16	
17	\begin{document}
18	\maketitle
19	
20	\begin{abstract}
21	As large language model (LLM) outputs accumulate on the web, training corpora increasingly contain recursively generated text of unknown provenance depth.
22	We formulate \emph{ordinal depth estimation}---classifying whether text is human-written (gen-0), first-generation synthetic (gen-1), or deeper (gen-2+)---a task that moves beyond binary real-versus-synthetic detection.
23	Existing detectors provide only binary scores and, when repurposed for ordinal prediction, exhibit high cross-generator variance (confidence intervals spanning 0.41--0.82), making them unreliable depth estimators.
24	We characterize a \emph{differential depth response} in distributional features computed under a held-out reference model: central-tendency and diversity features (entropy mean, type-token ratio, hapax ratio) drift monotonically with generation depth, while distributional shape features (entropy standard deviation, tail decay rate) remain near-stationary.
25	Combining both feature types into a 7-feature ensemble exploits this complementarity: the full set (macro-F1\,=\,0.79) outperforms surface-only (0.73) and reference-only (0.75) subsets.
26	In leave-one-generator-out evaluation across 6 generators from 3 model families (0.6B--8B parameters), the ensemble achieves macro-F1 of 0.79 ($p < 0.001$ vs.\ all single-detector baselines), with per-generator held-out F1 ranging from 0.68 to 0.86.
27	However, depth-aware data curation does not improve downstream language modeling quality compared to random subsampling---data quantity, not depth composition, is the primary determinant---positioning depth fingerprinting as a diagnostic tool for provenance characterization, not a curative tool for data selection.
28	\end{abstract}
29

Template\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\n\\graphicspath{{../chart/}}\n\n\\title{Depth Fingerprinting: Ordinal Generation Depth Estimation\\\\in Non-Paraphrastic LLM Text Chains}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n\\begin{abstract}\nAs large language model (LLM) outputs accumulate on the web, training corpora increasingly contain recursively generated text of unknown provenance depth.\nWe formulate \\emph{ordinal depth estimation}---classifying whether text is human-written (gen-0), first-generation synthetic (gen-1), or deeper (gen-2+)---a task that moves beyond binary real-versus-synthetic detection.\nExisting detectors provide only binary scores and, when repurposed for ordinal prediction, exhibit high cross-generator variance (confidence intervals spanning 0.41--0.82), making them unreliable depth estimators.\nWe characterize a \\emph{differential depth response} in distributional features computed under a held-out reference model: central-tendency and diversity features (entropy mean, type-token ratio, hapax ratio) drift monotonically with generation depth, while distributional shape features (entropy standard deviation, tail decay rate) remain near-stationary.\nCombining both feature types into a 7-feature ensemble exploits this complementarity: the full set (macro-F1\\,=\\,0.79) outperforms surface-only (0.73) and reference-only (0.75) subsets.\nIn leave-one-generator-out evaluation across 6 generators from 3 model families (0.6B--8B parameters), the ensemble achieves macro-F1 of 0.79 ($p < 0.001$ vs.\\ all single-detector baselines), with per-generator held-out F1 ranging from 0.68 to 0.86.\nHowever, depth-aware data curation does not improve downstream language modeling quality compared to random subsampling---data quantity, not depth composition, is the primary determinant---positioning depth fingerprinting as a diagnostic tool for provenance characterization, not a curative tool for data selection.\n\\end{abstract}\n\n\\input{introduction}\n\\input{related_work}\n\\input{method}\n\\input{results}\n\n\\input{discussion}\n\\input{conclusion}\n\\input{limitations}\n\\input{ethics}\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\appendix\n\\section{Length <details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YU4AuLpeoZrWJjwcpvqQAf`</summary>

```text
1	% Abstract — Depth Fingerprinting of Recursive LLM Generation Chains
2	\begin{abstract}
3	As large language model (LLM) outputs accumulate on the web, training corpora increasingly contain recursively generated text of unknown provenance depth.
4	We formulate \emph{ordinal depth estimation}---classifying whether text is human-written (gen-0), first-generation synthetic (gen-1), or deeper (gen-2+)---a task that moves beyond binary real-versus-synthetic detection.
5	Existing detectors provide only binary scores and, when repurposed for ordinal prediction, exhibit high cross-generator variance (confidence intervals spanning 0.41--0.82), making them unreliable depth estimators.
6	We characterize a \emph{differential depth response} in distributional features computed under a held-out reference model: central-tendency and diversity features (entropy mean, type-token ratio, hapax ratio) drift monotonically with generation depth, while distributional shape features (entropy standard deviation, tail decay rate) remain near-stationary.
7	Combining both feature types into a 7-feature ensemble exploits this complementarity: the full set (macro-F1\,=\,0.79) outperforms surface-only (0.73) and reference-only (0.75) subsets.
8	In leave-one-generator-out evaluation across 6 generators from 3 model families (0.6B--8.2B parameters), the ensemble achieves macro-F1 of 0.79 ($p < 0.001$ vs.\ all single-detector baselines), with per-generator held-out F1 ranging from 0.68 to 0.86.
9	However, depth-aware data curation does not improve downstream language modeling quality compared to random subsampling---data quantity, not depth composition, is the primary determinant---positioning depth fingerprinting as a diagnostic tool for provenance characterization, not a curative tool for data selection.
10	\end{abstract}
11	
```

_source line 3160 · 2026-05-15T08:45:42.809Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Ner4Yfbhe2jbXzHpAhYjH2`</summary>

```text
1	We complement the permutation importance analysis (\S\ref{sec:results-differential}) with a leave-one-feature-out (LOFO) ablation. Each row drops one of the seven features and re-evaluates the full 6-gen LOGO protocol; $\Delta$ is the change relative to the full 7-feature ensemble (F1\,=\,0.789).
2	
3	\begin{table}[h]
4	\centering
5	\small
6	\begin{tabular}{lcc}
7	\toprule
8	\textbf{Dropped feature} & \textbf{LOGO F1} & $\boldsymbol{\Delta}$ \textbf{vs Full} \\
9	\midrule
10	entropy\_max    & 0.774 & $-$1.50\,pp \\
11	entropy\_mean   & 0.783 & $-$0.63\,pp \\
12	ttr             & 0.784 & $-$0.55\,pp \\
13	hapax\_ratio    & 0.785 & $-$0.42\,pp \\
14	tail\_decay     & 0.786 & $-$0.33\,pp \\
15	entropy\_std    & 0.786 & $-$0.29\,pp \\
16	entropy\_min    & 0.789 & $+$0.01\,pp \\
17	\midrule
18	Full (7 features) & 0.789 & --- \\
19	\bottomrule
20	\end{tabular}
21	\caption{Leave-one-feature-out ablation (6-gen LOGO). Each row drops one feature and re-evaluates with the LOGO protocol. Sorted by impact magnitude.}
22	\label{tab:lofo}
23	\end{table}
24	
25	\paragraph{Analysis.}
26	Entropy\_max causes the largest degradation when removed ($-$1.5\,pp), despite ranking only fourth in permutation importance (split frequency 0.063). This indicates that entropy\_max provides unique signal---capturing the most surprising token under the reference model---that is not redundant with other features.
27	Conversely, entropy\_min is fully redundant ($\Delta$\,=\,+0.01\,pp), consistent with its near-zero permutation importance (0.015).
28	Six of seven features contribute positively to the ensemble, confirming that the 7-feature design is not over-specified.
29	
```

1	\section*{Ethics Statement}
2	\label{sec:ethics}
3	
4	All texts analyzed in this work are LLM-generated from publicly available Wikipedia seeds; no personal or sensitive data is involved.
5	Our method estimates the generation depth of a text passage---how many recursive generation steps it has undergone---not the identity of its author.
6	Individual authorship attribution is outside both the scope and the capability of this approach; privacy implications are minimal.
7	We acknowledge that depth estimation tools could in principle be used to selectively suppress machine-generated text in contexts where such content is legitimate (e.g., accessibility tools, assistive writing).
8	We encourage deployment in data governance pipelines where provenance transparency---rather than content removal---is the objective.
9

```text
1	% Appendix A — Length Confound Analysis
2	% Data source: plan-013 (23,032 samples, 5 generators)
3	
4	Surface diversity features (TTR, hapax ratio) mechanically depend on text length:
5	shorter texts yield higher type-token ratios by construction.
6	If generation depth systematically shortens text, observed depth trends could be length artifacts rather than genuine distributional shifts.
7	We rule this out with three complementary analyses.
8	
9	\paragraph{(a) Length--depth correlation.}
10	We compute Spearman rank correlation between word count and depth across all five generators.
11	Correlations are weak and inconsistent in sign:
12	$\rho$ ranges from $-0.066$ (Pythia-1B) to $+0.134$ (OLMo-2-1B), with $|\rho| < 0.14$ in every case.
13	One generator (Qwen3-0.6B) shows no significant correlation ($\rho = 0.012$, $p = 0.43$).
14	Length and depth are thus near-independent in our corpus.
15	
16	\paragraph{(b) Length-normalized feature trends.}
17	To eliminate residual length effects, we recompute TTR and hapax ratio on fixed 200-word windows.
18	After normalization, monotonic depth trends are fully preserved for all generators; moreover, Spearman correlations between normalized features and depth \emph{increase} in absolute value (e.g., Pythia-2.8B TTR: $\rho = -0.633 \to -0.715$).
19	Length removal sharpens the depth signal rather than diminishing it, confirming that the signal is not a length artifact.
20	
21	\paragraph{(c) Length-matched classification.}
22	We subsample the dataset so that word-count distributions are equalized across depth classes, then retrain the 3-class XGBoost classifier (\S\ref{sec:results-classification}).
23	Macro-F1 changes by $\Delta = +0.041$ relative to the unmatched baseline---performance \emph{improves} when length variance is removed.
24	This is consistent with length acting as noise rather than signal.
25	
26	\paragraph{Conclusion.}
27	We observe a dropout bias: shorter texts are more likely to fall below the minimum-length threshold at deeper generation stages.
28	However, this bias does not drive the depth fingerprint.
29	All three analyses converge on the same conclusion: the depth signal captured by our feature ensemble is independent of text-length variation.
30	
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01NWrEx3mS1QaR5vF5RN52Ei`</summary>

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Our experiments include generators up to 8.2B parameters (Qwen3-8B).
5	Depth signal persists at this scale, but cross-scale classifier transfer incurs a moderate gap (held-out F1\,=\,0.683 vs.\ 0.76--0.86 for $\leq$2.8B generators).
6	Frontier models (70B+) remain untested; their higher fluency may further compress feature margins.
7	
8	We evaluate three prompt types (continuation, elaboration, rewriting; Appendix~\ref{app:prompt-type}) but all share the same Pythia-2.8B generator.
9	Cross-prompt-type transfer (LOPTO F1\,=\,0.753) confirms the depth signal generalizes, yet additional prompt types (e.g., summarization, instruction following) and cross-generator $\times$ cross-prompt interactions remain untested.
10	
11	Chains are constructed with a single generator model per step.
12	Real-world web contamination likely involves heterogeneous generators at each depth, which could attenuate or alter the depth signal we observe.
13	We also do not evaluate robustness to human editing or post-processing of generated text, which is common in practice and may disrupt distributional features.
14	
15	The negative downstream result (\S\ref{sec:results-downstream}) is evaluated on causal language modeling only.
16	Other downstream tasks (classification, summarization, instruction following) may respond differently to depth composition.
17	
18	We use 3-class granularity (gen-0\,/\,gen-1\,/\,gen-2+). A 5-class evaluation (Appendix~\ref{app:finer-depth}) yields LOGO F1\,$\approx$\,0.50, with depths 2--4 mutually indistinguishable, validating the 3-class design as the practical resolution limit.
19	
```

</details>
